The Wrong Passenger","volume":"婚后外传：河岸集市失窃案","anchor":"chapter-0122","paragraphs":["Mara reached the canoe just as Lyle pushed it off. She stepped onto the fore seat, caught the river rail and stayed balanced while he stared at her. \"You booked the wrong passenger,\" she said. Behind them Cassian ran down the landing carrying his booby prize. He left the cabbage on a post, seized the rope and stopped the boat before it entered the current below the dam.","The parcel contained a fake daguerreotype. Its mount carried a neat engraving copied from the catalogue, but a modern scratch crossed a supposedly old repair. Lyle said it was a rehearsal object for the fair. Mara asked why a rehearsal needed a clandestine river journey. Beyond the billboard, the old factory courtyard was now abuzz with spectators, and he abruptly became gracious. He invited them to discuss the matter in the cocktail lounge.","Cassian preferred the open yard. A gun and its tethered bullet lay on the actor's table; the striped prop bomb stood beside them. Nothing there justified real alarm. Lyle nevertheless shouted that a bomb had been found. A visitor dropped a disposable cup. The keeper moved the capuchin away from the sudden crowd, and the concert steward calmly separated people from the electrical equipment. The emergency remained controllable because someone had practised the drill.","\"Don't allow him to coax you into a performance,\" Mara told Ada. \"He wants witnesses to remember confusion.\"","Lyle retreated into the converted barn. Mara followed a cleaner's damp footprints to the elevator, where cleanser had spilled against a clamp. A hamper caught beneath the door prevented it closing. Above them, the hoist began moving. Cassian cut power at the ordinary control switch; he did not touch the fuse box. The jerk loosened a baluster, which struck the floor beside Mara. Cassian pulled her against him, one hand cradling the back of her head.","\"You can let go now,\" she said when the noise stopped.","\"I'm considering a longer investment horizon.\"","\"Your returns look favourable.\"","The missing original was not on the platform. Instead, they found baggage booked to the helicopter and a forged embassy shipping label. Ada recognised her grandmother's handwriting on another envelope inside. It contained a letter about parent-child kinship: the child in the picture was an adopted nephew, not a secret son. A private letter was not his commodity to sell. That mattered to the family, but it was not the scandal Lyle had promised to sell. He had hoped to forge a spectacular history and make the original quietly vanish.","At the bookstore, a volunteer supplied the old catalogue. The aeroplane on its cover had the wrong wing for the actual aircraft in Ada's photograph. An airplane enthusiast explained the difference using a blackboard. The guide called it a classic design, with an unusually elevated wing. A child offered a paper model; Cassian declined to fly it again. An auto museum next door held an engraving of the same aircraft, independently dated. The aggregate evidence was now more useful than any one exciting discovery.","They still needed the mounting record under the flagstone. The path crossed the camp and passed the cottage, where the dryer had finished with Mara's coat. Cassian brought it out warm and settled it over her shoulders. She slipped her hands into his pockets as they walked. Beyond the chapel, an old chimney leaned against the sky. A sign warned that loose masonry could choke the narrow service route, so they used the marked path instead.","The forge had become a workshop. An axe hung above the bench, and the carpenter was repairing a chair with a drill. Mara borrowed neither; the stone had a lifting ring. Under it sat a tin containing a compass, a sealed paper capsule and the missing record. Ada's grandmother had made the hiding place decades earlier, then forgotten to tell her granddaughter. The recovered paper specified the plate's distinctive mount and noted an accidental corner mark. Lyle's fake lacked both.","\"I view his offer as an attempt to buy silence,\" Ada said. \"But the choice must rest with me until I authorise another loan.\"","The local policymaker agreed to call headquarters. A graphic timeline would help explain the duplication of the invoice. A federal institution had helped fund conservation work, so the contracts needed checking too. An influential donor complained about the time-consuming interruption. Ada gave him the upfront account he had requested. The restoration budget was relatively small; his assistant's payment to Lyle was not. Lyle needed a dose of realism. A sane response required receipts, not an overly dramatic accusation against everyone in the village.","Back at the old gaol, Mara played the cassette again. The slogans from his days on the ultra-right faded beneath an accidental conversation recorded after the public meeting. A constituent asked where the original plate would be stored. The reply named the cathedral archive. A non-celebrity volunteer at the guitar stall remembered bringing the loan in. The nostalgic exhibit was therefore holding a later loan, not the only surviving record of ownership. Ada had another route to verify it. Harvard University was mentioned in the subsequent correspondence, but no eminent name could substitute for an actual signed document.","Lyle found them beneath the cigaret advertisement, an unlit cigar clamped between his teeth. His quasi-official badge had disappeared. \"You will destroy economic activity here,\" he said. \"People will migrate to the city.\"","\"You tried to sell our property,\" Ada replied. \"You don't get to describe that as care.\"","A proposed settlement offered one buck for the imitation and a silence clause for the original. \"Terrific fiction,\" Mara said. \"A shocking contract.\"","She asked the trustees to stipulate independent inspection. A safe, economical arrangement could broaden the fair's reputation, while keeping the cozy dorm and every dwelling available to residents. No one had to put a quart into a pint pot by promising instant prosperity. Ada chose to stick with the truth.","Then the volunteer at the highway gate called. Lyle's assistant had loaded a square case onto the helicopter shuttle. Mara looked from Cassian to the departing van. He was already reaching for the keys."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1021,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0122\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：河岸集市失窃案</div><h1>2. The Last Prize","volume":"婚后外传：河岸集市失窃案","anchor":"chapter-0123","paragraphs":["Cassian caught the shuttle at the highway barrier without breaking a speed limit. A bridge closure was an excellent ally. While Lyle argued with the driver, Mara opened the baggage compartment with the driver's permission and found the square case. Its shipping label named an embassy; its contents were booked as an agricultural commodity. Ada's original daguerreotype lay inside, wrapped in a child's drawing of an aeroplane.","The assistant tried to claim a mistake. He offered to buy the fake back for a buck, as if a trivial price could make the deception harmless. The cassette of his agreement with Lyle made that difficult. So did the duplicate invoice found in the case. Duplication had been charged twice, first as restoration and again as transport. The aggregate amount exceeded the entire fair's expected proceeds. The policeman who arrived did not promise anyone gaol immediately. He secured the records and asked Lyle to accompany him. The quasi-official badge did not assist its former owner.","Mara returned the compass from the envelope, then the sealed capsule with the old label. Ada cried when she saw the corner mark on the plate. She had spent years feeling embarrassed by the scratched name, imagining some family disgrace. Now she could ask her living relatives about it. Any decision would rest with them: they could choose how much to share, without a producer turning their history into a shocking secret.","At the factory, the helicopter lifted away without Lyle. The airplane display resumed beside the camp. A child asked Cassian to demonstrate his paper flight again, and Mara defended his reputation by taking the controls. Her model crossed the blackboard and landed inside the booby prize cabbage. \"A terrific improvement,\" he said. \"I shall view the cabbage as a family heirloom.\"","The fair's reopening had to wait for one practical repair. The fallen baluster had damaged a bracket, so the carpenter used a clamp and a drill while the electrician checked the elevator and its fuse. Mara held the work light. Cassian helped operate the unloaded hoist under supervision, following each signal rather than assuming that title or strength made him qualified. The old barn had been built for grain, not crowds, and the trustees agreed to stipulate a capacity limit.","An axe returned to its locked rack. The stage gun and painted bullet went back into the actor's box; the striped bomb was retained for a comedy in the chapel. Lyle's false alarm had been ugly, but the volunteers refused to let it own the afternoon. A guitarist began to compose a tune about an archivist who defeated a thief with a mounting record. Ada requested fewer verses about the duke's cabbage.","The capuchin watched from its secure carrier while its keeper packed the last display. Mara helped coax a small visitor away from tapping the bars. The child instead drew the monkey on a disposable sheet. Nearby, a volunteer cleaned the fore deck of a rowing boat. The canoe club took donations toward a safer landing above the dam. No one promised that a new dock could repair everything. It could make one useful journey safer, which was enough reason to begin.","A local policymaker came to help move tables. One constituent demanded an elevated promise that such dishonesty would never happen again. \"We can promise checks we actually perform,\" Ada replied. At headquarters, the trustees published the contracts and the invoices. A federal institution would decide its own funding response. The initial results were less theatrical than Lyle's proposed scandal and considerably better for the village.","The bookstore sold out of the classic local history. An engraving of the cathedral raised enough for the library roof. At the old gaol, Ada added a note beside the cigaret advertisement explaining how the former councillor's campaign on the ultra-right had distorted the housing argument. The nostalgic exhibition kept the inconvenient recording too. Harvard University correspondence was displayed beside the ordinary village letters, each source identified. Famous stationery was interesting; clear provenance mattered more.","Mara washed the last trace of cleanser from her hands in the cottage kitchen. A dose of soap did the job. The dryer had swallowed one of Cassian's socks, and he regarded the machine with suspicion. \"A second theft,\" he said.","\"The evidence suggests negligence in pairing.\"","\"I hoped for a more supportive investigator.\"","She found the sock inside his rolled sleeve. He paid her with a kiss that left them both leaning against the warm cupboard, laughing softly when the kettle interrupted. Their cozy supper was delivered in the recovered hamper. This time it held bread rather than a forged record.","Before evening, they walked past the repaired chimney. Smoke rose normally from the cleared flue; it would no longer choke the workshop. At the forge, the flagstone had been reseated so nobody would trip. The old tin was now in the archive, and the surviving photograph rested in controlled storage. Mara refused to expose it merely because an overly eager visitor wanted a selfie. A fragile original should not have to earn its own protection every afternoon.","Lyle's auto remained in the police yard. His cigar had been left unlit on a bench. Ada used the empty space beside the billboard for an array of children's pictures rather than another sponsor slogan. She had been tempted to lose control when he threatened the fair. Instead, she had taken a step that made the small institution stronger. She was no longer helpless before a producer with a graphic presentation and a loud voice. The financial shortfall still needed work, but a non-celebrity volunteer had already offered to manage the receipts.","At work on the accounts, the volunteers found no further unexplained payments. The cocktail lounge opened for the evening concert, leaving the dorm quiet for those who wanted sleep. Mara and Cassian chose the river path. A lamp shone in each dwelling beyond the water. From the little bridge they could hear guitar music, a cheerful argument about the cabbage, and Ada laughing without apology.","\"Worth a weekend?\" he asked.","She rested her head on his shoulder. \"Ask me after breakfast. I have hopes of a prize-winning husband.\"","\"Too late. The Puppet in the Bridal Suite","volume":"婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜","anchor":"chapter-0124","paragraphs":["The hotel promised a honeymoon atmosphere. It supplied a puppet in the bridal suite. Cassian found the little astronomer on his pillow, its wooden face turned accusingly toward his open purse. A note explained that the playhouse company welcomed guests with a complimentary performance. Mara made the puppet bow. \"At last, a leading man I can fit in my baggage.\"","\"I am willing to fold.\"","She kissed him before he could demonstrate.","The coastal village had built a modest hub around its old observatory. A motel near the overpass accommodated performers, while the hotel hosted a jobcentre fair. Visitors could try a loom in the craft hut or learn to knit with Iris, the costume maker. Her lace cuffs had survived twenty years of touring. Today's show would help pay to pave the rough path between the station and the playhouse.","In the foyer, an ancestral saddle stood beneath a ceremonial spear. The saddle was a touring relic, on loan from the stadium exhibition. A retired fisherman demonstrated sonar beside a model spaceship, explaining why sound helped beneath water but could not cross empty space. Upstairs, an engineer used a magnet to guide a tiny satellite over a painted globe. The objects had purposes beyond decoration. At the newsstand, Mara bought the programme because Iris's portrait on the cover made her laugh: a fearsome heroine holding a darning needle.","Iris was less cheerful in person. Dane, the hotel manager, had declared the theatre unsafe and refused access to the power shed. He proposed a short-lived replacement show in the car park, then permanent conversion of the playhouse into storage. His leading argument was the roof; a builder said only a small patch needed repair. The council's monument officer had not authorised demolition. \"I attach importance to actual inspections,\" Mara said. \"A worried face is not a report.\"","Inside the theatre, a papier-mâché megalith blocked the aisle. Mara and Cassian moved it onto a mat while Iris repaired a slit in a dancer's pants. A wooden ski awaited its winter scene; Iris hoped to remake the matching prop. A missing stocking lay beneath a pottery moon. A sneaker dangled from the puppet stage, left by Pip, a teenage volunteer who had tried to skate during rehearsal. He was now using a rag to clean the floor and vigorously denying that his fall had harmed the roof.","Dane arrived holding a glossy proposal. He wanted everyone to shell out for an ultra-modern event pavilion. Its artist's impression showed modernism as a glass box, with no space for the community loom or the little stage. The declared masterpiece of design cost far more than repairs. \"Why overpay for that?\" Iris asked. The television behind him discussed the stock market. Dane became non-committal, then said her political view was irrelevant. She had asked about a price, not an election.","A storm warning interrupted them. The radar image showed rain approaching the coast. At the small runway, the last plane was preparing to leave; a pilot secured its parachute demonstration rig. The transport display had already closed. Cassian helped guide the display pony, holding one rein loosely. A rocket model was covered against the weather, and a museum missile was behind a barrier. Pip tried to shield a cardboard reactor with his jacket. Water would ruin the labels, though there was no fuel or hazard inside the model.","The caretaker needed access to the shed to inspect the emergency pump. Dane refused to part with the key. \"I will not get anywhere if every visitor demands a tour,\" he said.","\"He's your caretaker,\" Mara replied.","Cassian asked Dane to restate his reason. The manager said the lock was broken. Mara suspected a sham excuse. A moment later his assistant opened it to fetch wine. Through the doorway Mara saw a heavy plug pulled clear of its socket. The pump cable was coiled behind boxes marked for private sale. If water entered the underpass, the public road could flood.","They could not overload the upstairs supply with an extension cable. An electrician would have to inspect the correct circuit. Iris ran to telephone one; the internet had failed. Cassian used the hotel landline to call the council emergency desk. Mara photographed the disconnected cable, including a staple that still held the removal instruction. That snapshot might matter later. At the photocopier, she made a copy before Dane could demand the only sheet.","The first rain came sideways. A passenger car stopped below the overpass, its driver unable to see. A girl in the craft hut cried because her grandfather had not returned from the jetty. The village first-aid team opened its medical bag. A paramedic checked the sealed packets of lancets and the morphine stock record while another volunteer prepared blankets. They did not need a dramatic diagnosis to see that people should come indoors.","\"We must build up shelter space,\" Iris said. The hotel could accommodate families, but Dane wanted a deposit from each. His attempt to make the crisis profitable was controversial even among his own staff. Mara told him to uphold the emergency arrangement he had signed with the council. She had read it while waiting by the photocopier.","Cassian found an old paddle beside the kayak rack. He would use it to guide a loose board away from the drain, not attempt a grand river voyage. Mara slipped a rod through the gate catch and lifted it from the inside. Water rushed along the gutter. If the pump could be restored, the road might remain open.","\"We cannot fight against the weather,\" she said, \"but we can stop helping it.\"","Iris gathered the company to implement a shelter plan. Someone would stitch the torn curtain into a screen; someone else could stencil directions onto packing card. They could stock up on essential supplies when deliveries resumed. Pip must not lose control of the loaded scenery cart. Tea would quench fear less effectively than information, but it would help cold hands. The jobcentre adviser moved her test-taking skill workshop upstairs. The informal class was stimulating even for a high achiever, who needed a dry place to think.","Then a voice called from the jetty: the old fisherman was stranded beyond a fallen railing. Mara caught Cassian's eye. Their comfortable weekend had acquired a second act."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1052,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0124\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜</div><h1>1. A Second Act in the Rain","volume":"婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜","anchor":"chapter-0125","paragraphs":["Mara reached the jetty with a coil of rope, a life jacket and an expression that made Cassian abandon any thought of leaving her behind. The fisherman was on a low platform beyond the break. The water was cold and rising, but the coastguard had told them to stay back from its edge. Cassian looped the rope around a sound post. Mara used a long rod to pass the other end to the old man, who secured it while they waited for the rescue crew.","A loose paddle knocked against the ladder. Farther inland, water trapped a passenger car beneath the overpass. Its occupants had already reached the hotel. That mattered more than saving the vehicle. The stadium offered extra shelter. The village was a hub of frightened movement, but the emergency desk kept rescue assignments separate from volunteers' enthusiasm. \"We must put emphasis on shelter,\" Mara told Pip when he proposed swimming. \"Not on an impressive funeral.\"","At the playhouse, Iris had turned the papier-mâché megalith into a screen for changing wet clothes. A mat protected the dry floor. She found pants for one visitor and a stocking for another, then wrapped a child in a parachute canopy that had never been used for jumping. The museum curator confirmed it was a retired display cloth. It was not borrowed rescue equipment. Iris paused to knit a missing cuff, then closed a slit in the wet curtain with a quick stitch. A puppet grinned from the rail above them, wearing a tiny pillow as a crown.","Mara took a rag and knelt beside the child. His sneaker had rubbed his heel. The little boy asked whether the spaceship outside would float. \"Probably badly,\" she said. \"Let's keep you in the building designed for people.\"","His mother laughed for the first time since arriving.","The paramedic had returned with the fisherman, whose ankle was badly hurt. She recorded the morphine she administered and monitored him while arranging transport. A lancet remained sealed in her bag; not every implement had to be used just because it was available. Mara shielded the examination area with a theatre flat painted as a monument. Cassian brought a chair for the fisherman's granddaughter, then sat beside her until her grandfather could speak to her.","Dane came in vigorously denying responsibility. He said the pump had failed through overload. The electrician, recently arrived from the motel, had found no such failure. The plug had been deliberately removed and its socket covered. Dane claimed he was trying to implement repairs. Was his promised masterpiece only a sham? Its ultra-modern drawing borrowed fashionable modernism without solving a practical problem. The removal instruction on the copied sheet said to clear the shed for a commercial buyer. It did not mention maintenance.","\"You will not get anywhere by changing the subject,\" Iris said.","Dane made a short-lived attempt to blame Pip. The jobcentre adviser had worked in the village for years. She knew that Dane's proposed pavilion depended on describing the playhouse as beyond repair. A closed theatre would also make his vacant property look like the only possible event space. It was a political view about public money, she said, that could be debated openly. Disabling a shared pump was another matter. She asked him to restate whether he had authorised the disconnection.","He refused and reached for Mara's purse, where he thought the original instruction was hidden. Cassian caught his wrist before he touched it. \"Ask,\" he said, very softly. \"Try starting there.\"","Mara produced the duplicate. The original was already with the council officer. The photocopier had done its modest heroic work. A staple hole and a dark line matched the wall copy in her snapshot. This was a satisfactory basis for questions, not an invitation to invent missing evidence. Dane's attempt to take the purse made the questions less friendly.","They moved the school workshop into the hotel dining room. Pip had been embarrassed by his slow reading; the adviser asked him to explain how he had assembled the show radar. His answer was clear and practical. \"A test-taking skill can be learned,\" she told him. \"It doesn't measure your whole ability.\" Iris watched him straighten, then asked him to remake the damaged stage diagram. The boy would not let anyone edit his measurements merely to fit a prettier diagram. He began with a stencil and finished by adding a bold arrow.","Outside, rain rattled the pottery moon. The engineer secured the model satellite and removed the magnet before a small child swallowed it. The rocket had shed its paper nose, and the missile case leaked at one corner. He protected the labels while the cardboard reactor dried in the craft hut. The collection could wait; nobody needed to risk a life for a model. At the observatory, a radio relay carried local updates because the internet remained down. No one pretended sonar could warn people on land.","They would fight against the flood damage tomorrow; tonight even this relic of a stage was shelter. Cassian returned soaked. Mara made him change behind the megalith screen, handing him dry pants with an extravagantly innocent face. \"I hear the leading actor needs a costume assistant.\"","\"She has become alarmingly efficient.\"","She pulled him down by his clean collar and kissed him once, firmly. The audience behind the screen applauded. Iris said the romance had a timeless quality, but the laundry bills were immediate.","By dusk the electrician had restored the pump safely. The team could now rein in the flooding near the runway, where a plane remained tied down. A horse from the display stable needed its saddle removed and a dry stall. Pip carried the ceremonial spear to storage so nobody would trip; the loom and lace stayed upstairs. A ski costume had dried on the rail. Nobody would skate on the wet stage. A carpenter promised to pave the final stretch of path when the soil dried.","The newsstand owner brought biscuits to quench the last of their hunger. They did not need to overpay a private supplier in panic. People agreed to stock up on drinking water for the next day, rather than strip one shop bare. The stock market report on an abandoned radio seemed absurdly remote. Here the staple necessities were shelter and care.","Iris looked at her damaged scenery. \"We may have to cancel.\"","Mara studied the dry room, the waiting puppet and the crowd that had just rescued its own show. \"Or we give them an ending worth staying for.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1089,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0125\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：海边剧场的暴雨夜</div><h1>2.
